
> Build a travel planner. Here are the details to build a travel planner. The
  goal is to first create a plan based on the below requirements. I have
  already created a plan using a different agent, first validate the plan and
  ask any questions on it
  Vayo
  Assignment
  Take-Home Exercise - Profile Builder
  Role: Full-Stack Engineer
  Estimated time: ~2 hours
  Stack: Your choice; we use Next.js + TypeScript, and you'll have the
  smoothest review if you stay close to that, but pick what lets you do your
  best work in 2 hours.


  Why we're asking you to do this
  We want to see how you build, not just what you build. In 2 hours you can't
  ship a polished product, and we're not asking you to. We're asking you to
  make a small, real thing well; with the same care for naming, structure,
  commits, and trade-off explanations that you'd bring to a feature on your
  first week at Atlas.

  You can (and should) use whatever AI coding tools you normally use. We're
  more interested in your judgment, taste, and clarity than your typing speed.
  The deliverable here is your process as much as the artifact.


  LLM Provider and Credentials
  We won't provide an API key; provider choice and integration are part of the
  exercise. Use any LLM you want. Make the provider/model configurable, keep
  secrets out of the repo, and document setup in your README so we can see
  which provider/model you used, what env vars are required, and what happens
  when credentials are missing or invalid.

  You don't need a paid key. Most major providers have free tiers or trial
  credits. OpenRouter has free models
  (https://openrouter.ai/collections/free-models) but they vary in quality and
  tool-call reliability. If you go with a flaky free model, that shows up in
  how robust your app is; document the trade-off.


  The Task
  Build a single-page web app called Profile Builder that helps a traveler
  create a durable "travel profile" through conversation.

  The shape of it:

  A chat interface where the user talks to the assistant about their travel
  preferences.

  As the conversation progresses, the assistant extracts structured fields and
  builds a profile.

  A read-only view in the UI that lists the current travel preferences. Side
  panel, section below the chat, separate page — your call on placement, but it
  should be part of the app, not a debug route.

  The profile persists across reloads.


  Must have
  A working chat UI with streamed assistant responses (tokens appear as they
  arrive).

  The assistant extracts structured profile data from the
  ──── (114 lines hidden) ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  ughly in the order it shapes our impression:

  The annotated prompts. Clarity of thought, willingness to push back on the
  model, good taste about when to write something yourself rather than
  prompting for it.

  Commit history. Small, focused commits with messages that explain the why.
  One giant "initial commit" tells us very little. We're are dogmatic about
  being able to read your work as a story.

  README. Setup steps that work. A short section on the decisions you made and
  the trade-offs you accepted. A short section on what you'd do with another
  day.

  Code structure. Where things live and why. Separation between transport,
  business logic, and UI. Naming that doesn't make us hover over things.

  Types and API contract. Are client and server speaking the same language? Are
  runtime boundaries (LLM output, user input, persisted data) validated? We're
  looking for taste, not maximum strictness.

  Streaming and AI integration. Does the streaming actually work end-to-end? Is
  the extraction robust to the LLM occasionally being weird? How do you handle
  errors and partial state? How does the tool call thread back into the
  conversation — does the agent actually use what getDestinationInfo returned,
  or does it ignore it?

  Conflict handling. Conflicts are a place where lazy implementations show up
  fast. We'll look for: do you detect the conflict at all, do you handle it in
  a way that respects the user, and is the implementation localized or
  scattered across the codebase?

  What you cut and why. Two hours is short on purpose. We want to see
  deliberate scope decisions, not panicked omissions.

  What you didn't build. A short list of known gaps and how you'd address them
  is a strong positive signal.

  Provider choice and configuration. We are not evaluating which paid model you
  have access to. We are evaluating whether you made a reasonable provider
  choice, kept credentials out of the repo, documented setup cleanly, and
  handled model/API failure modes thoughtfully.

  Be honest about what you didn't fully understand or verify. If you
  copy-pasted a chunk of code from a prompt without reading it carefully, say
  so. Don't ship code you can't explain.


  Questions
  If anything is unclear, make an assumption and record it or send us a note
  before you start rather than guessing.


  Submission
  Reply with:

  Repo link (public GitHub or GitLab)

  Transcript file(s)

  PROCESS.md (in the repo is fine)

  Approximate time spent. If you went over 2 hours, tell us where the time
  went; we'd rather know

  brief is strictest:

  1. Every must-have is covered:

       unknown ones

     compares the stored profile with what was just extracted and returns
     conflicts, not checks scattered through prompts and UI code.
  3. The destination result reaches the reply. The data getDestinationInfo
     returns has to be fed back into the model's turn so the follow-up question
     uses it.

     request bodies, and data read back from storage.

     .env.example, and missing or invalid keys show a clear error in the UI.
  6. The plan fits 2 hours. It should say explicitly what's cut, and leave room
     for the process deliverables: PROCESS.md, the exported transcripts, and
     README decisions.

● I've read the v1.2.0 plan. It's a solid plan, and its main call is right: cut
  the production infrastructure and prove the core loop first. But it has one
  process problem that would hurt the submission directly, plus a few gaps
  against the brief. Here's my review, then four questions.

  What's right (keep it)

  - Two model calls: one streams the chat reply, a second extracts profile data.
    That's easy to defend, and a failed extraction can't break the chat.
  - Direct SQLite, the zod schemas file, and refreshing the profile after each
    turn are the right scope for 2 hours.
  - "Preserve the old value and record the conflict" never silently overwrites,
    which is the core of the conflict requirement.
  - The deferred list (ORM, live server-pushed updates, timeline, CI) is a good
    "what we didn't build" section for the README.
  - Checkpoints V0.1–V0.6 map well onto small commits.

  Problems, by severity

  1. "Don't create the repo until Step D" works against the most-weighted
  deliverable. Reviewers read the commit history as a story. Run git init before
  the first line of code and commit at each checkpoint. Pushing to GitHub can
  wait, but local git can't. The earlier v0.1/v0.2 folders have no history, so
  I'd start a fresh repo and redo those checkpoints as real commits.

  2. Matching only known destination names breaks the "unknown destination"
  requirement. The brief says any mentioned destination must trigger
  getDestinationInfo and that null must be handled sensibly. If the app only
  looks for the five hardcoded names, then:
  - the tool never returns null;
  - "Lisbon" goes straight to the model, which will happily make up facts about
    it;
  - there are false matches ("Georgia", "Paris Hilton") and misses ("Kyoto,
    Japan").

  Fix: let the model call getDestinationInfo as a tool during the streamed
  reply. A null result goes back to the model as "no data, don't state facts,
  ask what draws them there." Name matching (case-insensitive, aliases) happens
  inside the function.

  3. The client can't tell when extraction has finished. Extraction runs after
  the reply has streamed, so "client refetches after the turn" can fetch the old
  profile. Fix: run extraction before closing the response, then send one final
  event on the same stream carrying the updated profile and conflicts. This
  also gets close to the nice-to-have of live profile updates.

  4. Extraction only sees the newest user message. If the assistant asks "Are
  you vegetarian?" and the user answers "Yes," there's nothing to extract. Fix:
  pass the previous assistant message along with the user's reply.

  5. The profile fields are never defined. The brief lists this as an explicit
  "your call," and it gets graded. I'd propose these fields, each stored with
  the quote it came from:
  - budgetTier
  - travelPace
  - dietary[]
  - interests[]
  - dislikes[]
  - accommodation
  - companions
  - preferredSeasons[]
  - tripLength
  - accessibility
  - homeBase
  - destinationsOfInterest[]

  6. Conflict detection relies entirely on the model and can never be resolved.
  - Proposed split: the model proposes updates, and one module, conflicts.ts,
    decides what counts as a conflict. Simple fields like budget get compared in
    code. Contradictions in meaning, like vegetarian vs. steakhouse, use a
    model-provided contradicts flag. That keeps the logic in one place, as the
    reviewers want.
  - With no way to resolve a conflict, the user can never change their mind.
    That's a weak answer to "respects the user."

  7. Provider setup and error handling are missing. Add env vars for the
  provider and model, a .env.example, and clear errors in the UI when the key is
  missing or invalid.

  8. Data read back from SQLite isn't validated. Persisted data is one of the
  boundaries the brief names. Parse the profile with zod when it's loaded.

  9. Nothing is budgeted for the process deliverables or the 2-hour limit.
  PROCESS.md, the transcript export, and the README decisions need about 25
  minutes reserved. Add 2–3 tests on conflict detection and profile merging,
  since those would hurt most if they broke.

  10. Minor: the plan doesn't say which SQLite library to use. I'd use
  better-sqlite3, which ships prebuilt binaries so the reviewer's 5-minute setup
  holds. I'd also add a small JSON logger for model and tool calls (about 10
  minutes), since it makes it visible that the tool actually ran.

● User answered Claude's questions:

● Thanks for closing the files. I'd already read the plan without needing Word

  Here's the revised plan with your four answers built in. Everything not listed

  Revised plan (v1.3)

  Setup
  - Fresh repo at C:\Users\rotat\Projects\profile-builder.
  - Run git init before any code, and commit at each checkpoint with messages
    that explain why. Push to GitHub at the end.
  - Stack: Next.js + TypeScript, the Vercel AI SDK with OpenAI, better-sqlite3,
    and zod.
  - Env vars: OPENAI_API_KEY, plus LLM_MODEL (default gpt-4.1-mini).
  - Missing or invalid key: a clear error card in the UI, and the server logs
    the reason.

  Turn lifecycle (POST /api/chat, one response stream)
  1. Validate the request with zod and save the user message.
  2. Stream the reply. The model can call the getDestinationInfo tool for any
     destination; a null result comes back as "no data, don't state facts, ask
     what draws them." Any open conflicts are added to the prompt so the
     assistant asks about them.
  3. Save the assistant message.
  4. Extraction call: structured output with the current profile, the previous
     assistant message, and the new user message. It returns proposed updates,
     each with the quote it came from.
  5. conflicts.ts (one module, which is where the tests go) sorts the updates:
     - Applied: new information, or the user explicitly saying "actually, I've
       changed my mind."
     - Conflict: an implicit contradiction. The old value is kept and a conflict
       is recorded.
  6. Send a final event on the stream with the updated profile and conflicts,
     then close. The UI updates without refetching. If extraction fails, the
     reply stands and the UI shows a small warning.

  Profile fields (each stores the quote it came from):
  - budgetTier
  - travelPace
  - dietary[]
  - interests[]
  - dislikes[]
  - accommodation
  - companions
  - preferredSeasons[]
  - tripLength
  - accessibility
  - homeBase
  - destinationsOfInterest[]

  Conflicts: a card in the UI with Keep or Replace buttons, backed by POST
  /api/conflicts/:id/resolve. The assistant also asks about open conflicts on
  the next turn.

  Validation: zod checks request bodies, model output, and data read back from
  SQLite.

  Rough time budget: 2h 10m in total.

  ┌───────────────────────────────────────┬──────┐
  │                 Work                  │ Time │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ Scaffold and streaming                │ 20m  │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ SQLite storage                        │ 15m  │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ Extraction                            │ 25m  │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ Destination tool                      │ 15m  │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ Conflicts and resolve buttons         │ 25m  │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ Tests and JSON logging                │ 10m  │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ README, PROCESS.md, transcript export │ 20m  │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ Traits, only if there's time          │ —    │
  └───────────────────────────────────────┴──────┘

  Assumptions I'm making unless you object:
  - An explicit correction ("actually I eat meat now") updates the field
    directly. Only an implicit contradiction (vegetarian, then booking a
    steakhouse) creates a conflict.
  - This session is the official transcript. Your earlier sessions with the
    other agent should be exported and included too, labeled. The brief wants
    all sessions, and the v1.2.0 review comes from one of them.

  Should I save this as docs/PLAN.md in the new repo as the first commit, then
  start V0.1? Your OpenAI key goes in .env.local, which is gitignored. I won't
  read or commit it.

● I've written the new plan, but it isn't committed yet because git isn't
  installed on this machine.

  The plan is at docs/PLAN.md (Projects/profile-builder/docs/PLAN.md), in the
  new C:\Users\rotat\Projects\profile-builder folder.

  What's in it:
  - What changed from v1.2.0 (§0): a table covering all 12 changes. It doubles
    as the main annotated decision for PROCESS.md.
  - Profile fields (§3): each field has a reason it belongs on a durable profile
    and stores the user's own words that produced it.
  - Typed API (§4): includes typed error codes and a final stream event carrying
    the updated profile, so the UI never shows a stale profile after a turn.
  - Conflict rules (§8): a rule table in one module. An explicit "I've changed
    my mind" updates the field; an implicit contradiction creates a conflict
    with Keep and Replace buttons, and the assistant asks about it on the next
    turn.
  - Testing (§10):
    - Unit tests (Vitest) on the conflict rules, destination lookup, extraction
      validation and the database.
    - 3 Playwright flows against a mock model: streaming and reload, known and
      unknown destinations, and a conflict end to end.
    - A 5-case check against the real model, run on demand with a key.
    - GitHub Actions CI that needs no secrets.
  - Build sequence (§11): fits 120 minutes. If we fall behind, things get cut in
    a stated order, and a few items are marked never-cut.

  Decisions I made that you didn't explicitly choose:
  - The mock model also works as a keyless demo. Reviewers can run the app with
    LLM_PROVIDER=mock and no OpenAI key. The plan says honestly that the
    mock-based tests prove the wiring, not the model's judgment; the real-model
    check covers that.
  - Built-in node:sqlite instead of better-sqlite3. You're on Node 26, and
    better-sqlite3 may need a C++ compiler there, which would break the 5-minute
    setup. The catch is that the app requires Node 22.13 or later.
  - Travel-identity traits are cut entirely. Your earlier answer was "build last
    if time allows," but trimming to 2 hours leaves no time, so they're listed
    under "With another day."

  Blocker before building: git needs to be installed. The commit history should
  start with this plan. Should I install it with winget install --id Git.Git -e?
  Afterwards I'd set your git name and email, run git init, and commit the plan
  as the first commit. What name should commits use? I'd use
  rotatachar@gmail.com as the email.

  Also, please export the session where the other agent wrote v1.2.0 and keep
  it. It goes into transcripts/ with the rest of the submission.

● The next steps, in order:

  1. Install git (blocking). Nothing gets committed until it's installed, and

● The reload test exposed a race: the client marks a turn finished on the SDK's
  "finish" event, which fires before SQLite saves the turn. I'm making the
  client send "finish" only after the save and having the test wait for the
  chat's ready signal.

       7 +/** The profile as the model should see it: values only, empty 
         +fields omitted. */                                             
       8 +export function compactProfile(profile: TravelProfile): Record<
         +string, string | string[]> {                                   
       9 +  const out: Record<string, string | string[]> = {};           
      10 +  for (const field of PROFILE_FIELDS) {                        
      11 +    if (isScalarField(field)) {                                
      12 +      const entry = profile[field];                            
      13 +      if (entry) out[field] = entry.value;                     
      14 +    } else {                                                   
      15 +      const values = profile[field].map((e) => e.value);       
      16 +      if (values.length) out[field] = values;                  
      17 +    }                                                          
      18 +  }                                                            
      19 +  return out;                                                  
      20 +}

● Now the mock extraction model, which follows simple keyword rules, and the
  provider hook for it:

● Update(src\server\llm\mock.ts)
  ⎿  Added 72 lines
       49    );
       50  }
       51  
       52 +// -----------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
       53 +// Extraction: keyword rules standing in for the model's judgm
          +ent.                                                          
       54 +// -----------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
       55 +                                                              
       56 +type MockUpdate = { field: string; op: "set" | "add" | "remove
          +"; value: string; evidence: string; explicitChange: boolean };
       57 +type MockContradiction = { field: string; existingValue: strin
          +g; candidateValue: string; evidence: string; reason: string };
       58 +                                                              
       59 +function between(text: string, tag: string): string {         
       60 +  return text.match(new RegExp(`<${tag}>([\\s\\S]*?)</${tag}>`
          +))?.[1] ?? "";                                                
       61 +}                                                             
       62 +                                                              
       63 +const KEYWORD_RULES: Array<{ pattern: RegExp; field: string; o
          +p: "set" | "add"; value: string }> = [                        
       64 +  { pattern: /\bvegetarian\b/i, field: "dietary", op: "add", v
          +alue: "vegetarian" },                                         
       65 +  { pattern: /\bvegan\b/i, field: "dietary", op: "add", value:
          + "vegan" },                                                   
       66 +  { pattern: /\bsolo\b/i, field: "companions", op: "set", valu
          +e: "solo" },                                                  
       67 +  { pattern: /\b(my|with) (partner|wife|husband)\b/i, field: "
          +companions", op: "set", value: "partner" },                   
       68 +  { pattern: /\bluxury\b/i, field: "budgetTier", op: "set", va
          +lue: "luxury" },                                              
       69 +  { pattern: /\bmid-?range\b/i, field: "budgetTier", op: "set"
          +, value: "midRange" },                                        
       70 +  { pattern: /\b(on a budget|cheap|backpack)/i, field: "budget
          +Tier", op: "set", value: "budget" },                          
       71 +  { pattern: /\b(slow|relaxed)\b/i, field: "travelPace", op: "
          +set", value: "relaxed" },                                     
       72 +  { pattern: /\bfood\b/i, field: "interests", op: "add", value
          +: "food" },                                                   
       73 +  { pattern: /\bhiking\b/i, field: "interests", op: "add", val
          +ue: "hiking" },                                               
       74 +  { pattern: /\bmuseums?\b/i, field: "interests", op: "add", v
          +alue: "museums" },                                            
       75 +  { pattern: /\bboutique hotels?\b/i, field: "accommodation", 
          +op: "add", value: "boutique hotels" },                        
       76 +];                                                            
       77 +                                                              
       78 +export function scriptExtraction(prompt: string): { updates: M
          +ockUpdate[]; contradictions: MockContradiction[] } {          
       79 +  const message = between(prompt, "traveler_message");        
       80 +  const updates: MockUpdate[] = [];                           
       81 +  const contradictions: MockContradiction[] = [];             
       82 +                                                              
       83 +  const explicitMeat = /\b(i eat meat now|no longer vegetarian
          +|not vegetarian anymore)\b/i.exec(message);                   
       84 +  if (explicitMeat) {                                         
       85 +    updates.push({ field: "dietary", op: "remove", value: "veg
          +etarian", evidence: explicitMeat[0], explicitChange: true }); 
       86 +  } else {                                                    
       87 +    for (const rule of KEYWORD_RULES) {                       
       88 +      const match = rule.pattern.exec(message);               
       89 +      if (match) updates.push({ field: rule.field, op: rule.op
          +, value: rule.value, evidence: match[0], explicitChange: false
          + });                                                          
       90 +    }                                                         
       91 +  }                                                           
       92 +                                                              
       93 +  const steak = /\bsteakhouse\b/i.exec(message);              
       94 +  if (steak) {                                                
       95 +    contradictions.push({                                     
       96 +      field: "dietary",                                       
       97 +      existingValue: "vegetarian",                            
       98 +      candidateValue: "",                                     
       99 +      evidence: steak[0],                                     
      100 +      reason: "You mentioned a steakhouse, but your profile sa
          +ys you're vegetarian.",                                       
      101 +    });                                                       
      102 +  }                                                           
      103 +                                                              
      104 +  return { updates, contradictions };                         
      105 +}                                                             
      106 +                                                              
      107 +export function createMockExtractionModel() {                 
      108 +  return new MockLanguageModelV4({

      112 +  get(): TravelProfile {

          + WHERE id = 1").get() as { data_json: string } | undefined;

      115 +    try {                                                     
      116 +      const parsed = TravelProfileSchema.safeParse(JSON.parse(
          +row.data_json));                                              
      117 +      if (parsed.success) return parsed.data;                 
      118 +      warnCorrupt("profile", parsed.error.message);           
      119 +    } catch (error) {                                         
      120 +      warnCorrupt("profile", error);                          
      121 +    }

          +ta_json, updated_at = excluded.updated_at`,                   
      130 +      )                                                       
      131 +      .run(JSON.stringify(TravelProfileSchema.parse(profile)),
          + new Date().toISOString());                                   
      132 +  },

● Update(src\shared\contract.ts)
  ⎿  Added 40 lines
       99  }
      100  
      101  // -----------------------------------------------------------
           ----------------
      102 +// Conflicts                                                  
      103 +// -----------------------------------------------------------
          +----------------                                              
      104 +                                                              
      105 +/**                                                           
      106 + * A new statement that contradicts an existing profile value.
          + The profile is                                               
      107 + * NOT changed until the traveler resolves it (button or chat)
          +.                                                             
      108 + */                                                           
      109 +export const ConflictSchema = z.object({                      
      110 +  id: z.string(),                                             
      111 +  /** Field the candidate value would go into. */             
      112 +  field: z.enum(PROFILE_FIELDS),                              
      113 +  /** Field holding the existing value — differs from `field` 
          +for cross-field                                               
      114 +   *  conflicts (e.g. liking something listed under dislikes).
          + */                                                           
      115 +  existingField: z.enum(PROFILE_FIELDS),                      
      116 +  existingValue: z.string(),                                  
      117 +  /** "" means "the existing value would simply be removed". *
          +/                                                             
      118 +  candidateValue: z.string(),                                 
      119 +  /** The traveler's words that triggered the conflict. */    
      120 +  evidence: z.string(),                                       
      121 +  reason: z.string(),                                         
      122 +  /** kept / replaced = resolved by the traveler; superseded =
          + a later explicit statement settled it. */                    
      123 +  status: z.enum(["open", "kept", "replaced", "superseded"]), 
      124 +  createdAt: z.string(),                                      
      125 +  resolvedAt: z.string().nullable(),                          
      126 +});                                                           
      127 +export type Conflict = z.infer<typeof ConflictSchema>;        
      128 +                                                              
      129 +/** POST /api/conflicts/:id/resolve body. */                  
      130 +export const ResolveConflictRequestSchema = z.object({ choice:
          + z.enum(["keep", "replace"]) });                              
      131 +export type ResolveConflictRequest = z.infer<typeof ResolveCon
          +flictRequestSchema>;                                          
      132 +                                                              
      133 +/** POST /api/conflicts/:id/resolve response. */              
      134 +export const ProfileStateSchema = z.object({                  
      135 +  profile: z.lazy(() => TravelProfileSchema),                 
      136 +  /** Open conflicts only. */                                 
      137 +  conflicts: z.array(ConflictSchema),                         
      138 +});                                                           
      139 +export type ProfileState = z.infer<typeof ProfileStateSchema>;
      140 +

      176 +function parseConflicts(rows: unknown[]): Conflict[] {        
      177 +  return rows.flatMap((row) => {                              
      178 +    const parsed = ConflictSchema.safeParse(row);             
      179 +    if (parsed.success) return [parsed.data];                 
      180 +    warnCorrupt("conflicts", parsed.error.message);           
      181 +    return [];                                                
      182 +  });

      185 +export const conflictsRepo = {                                
      186 +  listOpen(): Conflict[] {                                    
      187 +    return parseConflicts(                                    
      188 +      getDb().prepare(`SELECT ${CONFLICT_COLUMNS} FROM conflic
          +ts WHERE status = 'open' ORDER BY created_at`).all(),         
      189 +    );                                                        
      190 +  },                                                          
      191 +                                                              
      192 +  get(id: string): Conflict | undefined {                     
      193 +    return parseConflicts([getDb().prepare(`SELECT ${CONFLICT_
          +COLUMNS} FROM conflicts WHERE id = ?`).get(id)].filter(Boolean
          +))[0];                                                        
      194 +  },                                                          
      195 +                                                              
      196 +  insert(c: Conflict) {                                       
      197 +    getDb()

      199 +        `INSERT INTO conflicts (id, field, existing_field, exi
          +sting_value, candidate_value, evidence, reason, status, create
          +d_at, resolved_at)                                            
      200 +         VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)`,              
      201 +      )                                                       
      202 +      .run(c.id, c.field, c.existingField, c.existingValue, c.
          +candidateValue, c.evidence, c.reason, c.status, c.createdAt, c
          +.resolvedAt);                                                 
      203 +  },                                                          
      204 +                                                              
      205 +  resolve(id: string, status: Exclude<Conflict["status"], "ope
          +n">, resolvedAt: string) {                                    
      206 +    getDb().prepare("UPDATE conflicts SET status = ?, resolved
          +_at = ? WHERE id = ? AND status = 'open'").run(status, resolve
          +dAt, id);                                                     
      207 +  },                                                          
      208 +

      21 +export function resolveConflictById(id: string, choice: "keep" 
         +| "replace"): ProfileState | null {                            
      22 +  return transaction(() => {                                   
      23 +    const conflict = conflictsRepo.get(id);                    
      24 +    if (!conflict || conflict.status !== "open") return null;  
      25 +    const now = new Date().toISOString();                      
      26 +    const { profile, status } = resolveConflict(profileRepo.get
         +(), conflict, choice, now);                                    
      27 +    profileRepo.save(profile);                                 
      28 +    conflictsRepo.resolve(id, status, now);                    
      29 +    return { profile, conflicts: conflictsRepo.listOpen() };   
      30 +  });                                                          
      31 +}

● A runtime bug, where a same-value refresh with different casing broke the
  enum, showed that refreshes should keep the stored value and only update the
  evidence. The type errors come from untyped test fixtures, so I'll validate
  those through the schema instead.

       1 # Profile Builder
       2
       3 A single-page app where a traveler chats with an assistant and t
         he assistant builds a durable **travel profile** from the
       4 conversation. Replies stream token-by-token. Preferences are ext
         racted into a typed profile shown beside the chat. Destinations
       5 are looked up with `getDestinationInfo`. A statement that contra
         dicts the profile becomes a **conflict for the traveler to resol
         ve**,
       6 never a silent overwrite.

● The app is built and committed as 10 local commits in
  C:\Users\rotat\Projects\profile-builder. Every checkpoint passes typecheck,
  lint, 47 unit tests and 12 Playwright tests. One big caveat: the real OpenAI
  path has never run. There was no key in this session, so everything was tested
  against the scripted mock model. A fresh clone installs and passes typecheck
  and unit tests in about 35 seconds; E2E wasn't run on the clone.

  What works, checked by tests:
  - Streaming: the E2E test samples the reply bubble and sees it fill in over
    several steps.
  - Persistence: the conversation, profile and conflicts survive a reload. A
    turn that fails before any text is not saved, so "Try again" can't duplicate
    history.
  - Extraction: a second model call after each reply. Any proposed value that's
    invalid, or whose quote isn't actually in your message, is dropped and
    logged.
  - Destination tool: the model calls getDestinationInfo itself. For Kyoto the
    reply includes facts that exist only in the tool data ("$180 a day"). For
    Lisbon it says it has no data and gives no prices.
  - Conflicts: vegetarian followed by "best steakhouse?" shows a card and leaves
    the profile alone. You resolve it with Keep/Replace, or by answering the
    assistant, which asks about it on the next turn. All the rules are in one
    module, conflicts.ts.
  - No-key mode: LLM_PROVIDER=mock npm run dev runs the whole app without a key.
    I smoke-tested it in dev mode.

  Bugs the tests caught along the way (written up in PROCESS.md):
  - A reload right after a reply could lose the turn, because the "done" signal
    reached the browser before the save.
  - Re-stating a value in different casing ("LUXURY") broke the stored value.
  - "The steakhouse is for a friend" resolved the conflict and re-raised it in
    the same message.

  Where the build departed from the plan: each change and its reason is in the
  appendix of docs/PLAN.md. Two you should know about:
  - The user message is now saved together with the reply at the end of the
    turn, not first as the plan said.
  - Travel-identity traits were cut entirely, since you chose to trim to 2
    hours.

  What's left for you:
  1. Run the real model. Put your key in .env.local, then run npm run eval and
     try the app with npm run dev. Against the mock, the eval passes 9 of 11
     checks. The two failures (spotting "started eating meat again", inferring
     "budget" from hostels) need judgment the mock's keyword rules don't have,
     so I left them failing on purpose. Put the real-model results in
     PROCESS.md.
  2. Export the transcripts into transcripts/: this session (/export) and the
     other agent's session. The v1.2.0 plan doc is already copied there.
  3. Write the ✍️ sections of PROCESS.md: your reflection, which files you read
     closely, and time spent. I drafted the highlights from facts but didn't
     write your opinions for you.
  4. Check the commit author. Commits are authored as rotatachar
     <rotatachar@gmail.com>, set for this repo only. If you want a different
     name, it's easy to rewrite before pushing.
  5. Push to GitHub. I haven't pushed, since that makes the code public. Create

● The app builds and runs at http://localhost:3000, but the real model can't
  answer: your OpenAI account has no credits left. The key itself is valid.
  OpenAI accepted it and replied "You have no credits remaining."

  What I did:
  - Key: copied it from the v0.2 project into profile-builder/.env.local without
    printing it. Git ignores that file, so it can't be committed or end up in
    an exported transcript.
  - Build: the production build is clean, and every API route is marked dynamic,
    so none gets prerendered.
  - First live call: it reached OpenAI and got the no-credits error. It also
    exposed a bug: my error mapping treated the error OpenAI uses for "no
    credits" the same as a temporary rate limit, so the app said "busy, try
    again". I gave billing errors their own message ("account is out of
    credits"), added tests, and committed the fix. The running server now shows
    the correct message; I checked it live.
  - Still unverified: the real-model path (tool calls, extraction, conflicts)
    still hasn't produced a reply. npm run eval will fail the same way until the
    account has credits.

  Options:
  1. Add credits to OpenAI (platform.openai.com → Billing; a few dollars covers
     this whole exercise many times over). No code changes. Tell me when it's
     done, and I'll run a live turn and npm run eval and record the results in
     PROCESS.md.
  2. Use a free provider instead. Google Gemini's free tier would be my pick,
     since its tool calling is more reliable than most free OpenRouter models.
     It's a small change in provider.ts plus one package, and you'd need a free
     Gemini key from AI Studio.
  3. Click through now in mock mode while you decide: I restart the server with
     LLM_PROVIDER=mock. Everything works, but the replies come from a script.

      52 -**Why OpenAI `gpt-4.1-mini`:** the whole design leans on two ca
         -pabilities, reliable tool calls (destinations) and strict struc
         -tured                                                          
      53 -outputs (extraction). A small OpenAI model does both well at lo
         -w cost. Free OpenRouter models were the alternative; their     
      54 -tool-call reliability varies, which would show up directly as m
         -issed lookups and failed extractions. Swapping providers is a  
      55 -change in one file (`src/server/llm/provider.ts`).             
      53 +Keys live only in `.env.local` (gitignored) and are never logge
         +d.                                                             
      54  
      55 +**Why Gemini 2.5 Flash (with OpenAI as a drop-in):** the design
         + leans on two capabilities, reliable tool calls (destinations) 
      56 +and structured outputs (extraction). I started on OpenAI `gpt-4
         +.1-mini`, and the first live run failed because the account was
      57 +out of credits. That's how the `llm_quota_exceeded` handling be
         +low came about. Gemini 2.5 Flash has a free tier and solid     
      58 +tool calling and JSON-schema output, so a reviewer can run it a
         +t no cost. Free OpenRouter models were the other option, but   
      59 +their tool-call reliability varies, which would show up directl
         +y as missed lookups and failed extractions. **Trade-off:** the 
      60 +free tier allows only a few requests per minute, and each turn 
         +makes two calls (reply + extraction), so typing fast can hit   
      61 +the per-minute limit. The app reports that as a transient "wait
         + a moment" error, not a failure. Providers are swapped in one  
      62 +file (`src/server/llm/provider.ts`).                           
      63 +                                                               
      64  **Failure modes:**
      65

  - The app now supports LLM_PROVIDER=google. It reads
    GOOGLE_GENERATIVE_AI_API_KEY and defaults to gemini-2.5-flash; LLM_MODEL

    as a drop-in alternative.

    shows "API key rejected". The free tier's per-minute limit shows "wait a
    moment and try again", rather than the "out of credits" message, which is
    now reserved for daily-quota and billing problems. All 55 unit tests pass.
  - README now explains why Gemini, and the trade-off: each turn makes two calls
    (reply + extraction), and the free tier allows only a few requests per
    minute, so typing quickly can hit the limit.

  unit
    tests in 35s; E2E not run on the clone

  Not covered:
  1. Extraction fails or returns junk. The "Couldn't update your profile" notice
     and the one retry on malformed output are untested.
  2. Stream dies mid-reply. Keeping the partial reply marked "interrupted" and
     skipping extraction is untested.
  3. The tool itself throws. It's supposed to be treated as "not found";
     untested.
  4. The resolve endpoint's input checks. An invalid body (400) and an unknown
     or already-resolved conflict id (404) are untested; only the happy path
     runs, via E2E.
  5. A corrupt profile row in SQLite. It's supposed to fall back to an empty
     profile; only the corrupt-message case is tested.
  6. Conflict scenarios against the real model. The eval only covers steakhouse
     vs. vegetarian. Resolving a conflict by answering in chat, the assistant
     asking about it on the next turn, a single-value conflict (luxury →
     "hostels only") and the likes-vs-dislikes conflict are tested only with the
     mock model or unit tests, so the real model's judgment there is
     unmeasured.
  7. The nice-to-haves. The JSON logs and the "Updating…" indicator were checked
     by eye only.
  8. Two destinations in one message, e.g. "Kyoto or Bangkok?", is untested.

  My recommendation: items 1–5 are about 20 minutes. Each needs a mock trigger
  (like the existing [mock:fail]) or a small unit test, and they cover failure
  paths the brief explicitly evaluates ("how do you handle errors and partial
  state"). Item 6 is three more eval cases, around 10 minutes plus a few cents
  of API calls. Items 7–8 I'd list as known gaps.

  Should I add 1–6?

> 1. Streaming conversation

  The traveler chats naturally with the assistant and sees the assistant
  response appear progressively as it is generated.

  2. Structured extraction

  Conversation such as:

  "I usually stay in boutique hotels and spend more money on food than
  accommodation."

  needs to become structured profile information.

  3. Persistence

  Profile and conversation state must survive a browser reload.

  4. Visible travel profile

  The application includes a read-only view showing what it currently knows
  about the traveler.

  5. Typed contracts

  The data moving between browser, API, model extraction, and persistence has
  explicit schemas/types.

  6. Destination tool

  Whenever the conversation references a known destination, the app must call:

  TypeScript
  getDestinationInfo(name)

  The tool has hardcoded information for at least five destinations.

  Its information should influence the assistant's next question.

  7. Conflict detection

  The assistant must not silently rewrite contradictory profile data.

  Example:

  Plain Text
  Earlier:
  "I'm vegetarian."

  Later:
  "I plan my trips around steakhouses."

  The system should surface that potential contradiction rather than quietly
  changing the travel profile.

  8. Documentation

  Ultimately the repository needs a README and PROCESS document explaining how
  to operate the application and why important design decisions were made.

  real model, not rely on similar test cases. I'll run both against the running

  The three fixes, all prompt changes:
  1. Spending priorities now count as interests. "Spend more on food" becomes
     interests: food.
  2. The assistant must ask about open conflicts. An open conflict goes first in
     its instructions, and its question has to be about it; it may no longer
     explain contradictions away.
  3. Vague replies decide nothing. "Sounds good" in answer to an either/or
     question picks no option and doesn't resolve a conflict. Before, it had
     been recorded as travelPace: relaxed.

> the authentication from my authenticator does not seem to be be working, am i
  missing anything?

● Two codes are involved, and mixing them up is the usual problem. Check these
  in order:

  1. Which code goes where:
  - The terminal shows a one-time device code like ABCD-1234. Enter it at
    github.com/login/device. Your authenticator app doesn't produce it.
  - The 6-digit authenticator code is only asked for if GitHub makes you sign in
    first (username + password → 2FA). Enter it on that sign-in page, not in
    the terminal.

  2. If GitHub rejects the 6-digit code:
  - Your phone's clock has drifted. Authenticator codes depend on the time. Turn
    on automatic date and time on your phone (Google Authenticator also has
    Settings → Time correction for codes).
  - It's the wrong entry. If you have several GitHub accounts in the app, pick
    the one matching the account you're signing into.
  - The code expired. Codes change every 30 seconds; enter it right after it
    refreshes.
  - Other options: on the 2FA page, click "Use a recovery code" or "GitHub
    Mobile" if you set those up.

  3. Make it easier: first sign in to github.com normally in your browser, then
  run the login again. The device page will only ask for the device code. Device
  codes expire after about 15 minutes, so start again if it's been a while.

  One addition to the command: the repo includes a GitHub Actions workflow, and
  pushing workflow files needs an extra permission that GitHub's default login
  doesn't grant. Use:

  & "C:\Program Files\GitHub CLI\gh.exe" auth login --web --git-protocol https
  --scopes workflow

  If 2FA still won't work, use a token instead:
  1. On github.com, go to Settings → Developer settings → Personal access tokens
     → Tokens (classic) → Generate new token.
  2. Tick the repo, workflow and read:org scopes. (gh needs read:org to accept a
     token.)
  3. Run & "C:\Program Files\GitHub CLI\gh.exe" auth login --with-token and
     paste the token at its prompt. Paste it only in your terminal, not here:
     this chat goes into your public transcript.

  Which step does it fail at? Is the 6-digit code rejected, is there no 2FA
  prompt at all, or does the device page give an error?

> if we were to run this architecture in an agentic model with each taks run
  with its own agent, can we do that?

● Yes, it can. But splitting by task isn't automatically better, and for this
  app only part of it benefits. The reason is the core design choice: wherever
  possible, a decision is made by plain code, not by a model.

  The current tasks, and whether each should become an agent:

  Task today: Conversation (streamed reply + destination tool)
  As its own agent?: ✅ Already one: a model in a tool loop. It would become the

    orchestrator
  Why: It's the only part the user sees, so it owns streaming
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task today: Extraction (second model call)
  As its own agent?: ✅ Natural sub-agent: "Profile Extractor"
  Why: Already isolated, with its own prompt, schema and guards. As an agent it
    could also ask the orchestrator to clarify ambiguous input instead of
    dropping it
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task today: getDestinationInfo
  As its own agent?: ❌ Keep it a tool
  Why: It's a lookup. An agent here adds latency and a new way to invent facts,
    which the null path exists to prevent
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task today: Conflict decisions (conflicts.ts)
  As its own agent?: ❌ Keep it as code
  Why: It's deterministic, tested rule by rule, and auditable. A "conflict
  judge"
    agent would make the most-scrutinized behavior non-deterministic. The model
    already does the fuzzy part (spotting semantic contradictions); code makes
    the decision
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task today: Persistence / resolve endpoint
  As its own agent?: ❌
  Why: Plain data operations, no judgment involved
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task today: New: destination research
  As its own agent?: Maybe, later
  Why: Only worth it with real, messy data sources to search and summarize. With

    six hardcoded entries, no
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Task today: New: profile reviewer / summarizer
  As its own agent?: Maybe, at scale
  Why: Periodically compresses long histories and checks the profile for
    consistency, in the background, outside the chat turn

  What it would look like:

  User ──▶ Conversation agent (orchestrator, streams to UI)
             ├─ tool: getDestinationInfo         (function)
             └─ hands off each exchange ──▶ Extraction agent (structured output)
                                                └─▶ conflicts.ts (code decides)
  ──▶ SQLite

  AI SDK 7, which we already use, supports this directly: each role becomes its
  own tool-loop agent, and the orchestrator calls sub-agents as tools or hands
  work to them. Most of the change would be in turn.ts and provider.ts. The
  contract, rules, storage and UI wouldn't change, because the layers are
  already separated.

  What it would cost, and what it would buy:
  - Latency and cost: more model calls per turn, and agent-to-agent hops add
    seconds.
  - Testing: agents talking to agents are harder to test deterministically.
    Today the mock model and E2E tests give the same result every run.
  - A real latency win available today without agents: start extraction at the
    same time as the reply instead of after it. It only needs your message and
    the previous reply. The profile would update sooner and turns would finish
    faster.
  - Independent scaling: separate agents allow a cheaper model per role, plus
    separate retries and monitoring. That's useful at 10k requests an hour,
    which is the scenario PROCESS.md already sketches.

  My recommendation for the submission: don't re-architect now. The brief
  rewards deliberate scope decisions, and "rules in code, judgment in models" is
  one. A short "Multi-agent version" note in the README's "With another day"
  section shows you've thought it through. I can add that note, or prototype the
  parallel-extraction change if you'd like to see the latency difference.

  One thing I noticed: the file you just opened,
  2026-11-04-210510-build-a-travel-planner.txt, looks like the /export of this
  session. It was saved in the v0.2 folder, not in profile-builder\transcripts\.
  Move it to profile-builder\transcripts\02-claude-code.txt, add your Copilot
  Chat export next to it, and I'll check both for secrets, commit and push.

  would make it a reconstruction, which they explicitly say they don't want.

  your next /export as it happened. I'll add it to the README (as a design
  decision, plus a sketch under "With another day") and to PROCESS.md as an
  annotated highlight.
